ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-04-sejm-83-2.u6	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Polish fire protection system, the fire protection system consists, you can say, of two lungs. From the professional lungs of the State Fire Department and from the lungs of voluntary, social, voluntary firefighters. In Poland over 500 thousand occur in the course of the year. events to which professional or volunteer firefighters rush or together. It is easy to count that every minute someone in Poland needs their help. Of those 500,000. Half of the events are served by professional firefighters, and the other half by professional firefighters together with volunteers, or by volunteers themselves. So about 1/2, or 1/4 of all events is served by the volunteers themselves, and the other 1/4 of this half together with professional firefighters. So we take care of both lungs equally, because they are equally needed and working together, ensuring the safety of Poles in various situations, in situations of different oppression. Because today they are not only fires, but also various other so-called fires. Local threats, traffic accidents, actions of nature forces, civilizational threats, technical threats - these are different situations. And today, the fire department is responding to all of them. This is the most multifunctional of all services. Today we cannot imagine our safety in Poland without both professional firefighters and volunteers. The members of the boards of these banks have a duty, with responsibility, a very serious responsibility, to act in the best interest of the funds entrusted to them. After all, they have the resources, they lend the capital. Where's the capital coming from? This capital is from people who keep their money on deposits in these banks. Therefore, we cannot expect them to, say, make decisions as if in complete isolation from the risks associated with the situation. Therefore, we are dealing with a very complicated, risky trade transaction between private entities, Ukrainian, Russian capital and banks in which, yes, the Treasury has shares, but they must operate on commercial terms. Therefore, it seems to me that such - these were already more statements than what is in writing - demanding, saying that here the Polish government must guarantee this transaction, is really completely inappropriate. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-04-sejm-83-2.u21	Thank you very much. Mr Stanisław Szwed, Minister of State, is responsible for the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-04-sejm-83-2.u22	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you for that question, because it is absolutely timely. Since 1 July, we have implemented an extended benefit scheme, the Family 500+, i.e. benefits in the amount of 500 PLN per child. I think it is worth reminding here that this is practically the first programme in the history of our country, which helps Polish families in such a direct way. For us, the Government of Law and Justice, for the Government of the United Right, this was the most important commitment that we have made to our fellow countrymen and that we are consistently fulfilling. So far, the programme has been universal, more than 52%. Children, 1600 thousand. children benefited from this programme either without an income criterion, where there was a second, third child, or taking into account an income criterion with the first child. Today the income criterion no longer applies, i.e. all children up to the age of 18 are entitled to this benefit. Also, all children who are in other institutions, in foster care, in care centres, or in welfare homes - these children are also covered by the programme. A total of 6800 thousand. The children will benefit from this program. On 1 July the extended programme entered into force. From 1 July we can submit applications by electronic means, from 1 August - by paper. And this period, this first period for those who apply for the first time, is 23 months, until the end of May 2021, and for those of the old hand for which this period is still in place until the end of September, that is a little shorter. So on the one hand, we have an extended period, but on the other hand we will introduce a very good solution, which also worked out with a good start, i.e. with a school expedition. The point is that there is no administrative decision by local governments today, but there is information. As for those who submit applications by electronic means, the information goes to their e-mail mailboxes. If someone wants it in a paper version, they can of course go to the office that pays these benefits, and these are, I will remind you, in the offices of GOPS municipalities or ILOS, or other institutions that pay these benefits. This simplification has also led to a very short examination cycle. The proposal is also very simple, it has two pages on which we only write basic data about children. There is no longer an income criterion, i.e. there is no need to check the income of these families, and this has made it much easier to deal with these applications, but has also helped local governments in the sense that this speed of action is already much higher. These are the changes we've made. We have also made some other important changes, e.g. at birth a mother or parents have a period of three months to submit an application. So we also extend this period so that there is no such situation as sometimes, that someone did not have time to submit an application. Also, in a tragic situation where one parent dies, another parent is entitled to this benefit, another parent is no longer required to submit another application. These are the changes that have significantly facilitated the examination of these proposals. It is also worth recalling here that the aim of our programme was three things: investment in the family, and that is, I think, the widest formula, the issue of demographics and the issue of combating poverty. Poverty indicators. More than half fell, poverty rates among children decreased: from 9.7% Up to 4.7%. When we talk about the fertility rate, in 2015, the fertility rate was 1.29, i.e. 129 children were born out of 100 birthing mothers. In 2018 this indicator is already at 1.45, i.e. 145 children were born out of 100 mothers, which means we have an increase in the fertility rate. And an investment in human capital. More than 71 billion zlotys have already been sent to Polish families in the framework of this edition of the 500+ family programme, and these are funds that go directly to the families. Now, after the extension of the programme, the 500+ family will additionally receive PLN 10 billion this year, i.e. the total cost of the programme in 2019 will be PLN 31 billion, in 2020 - PLN 41 billion. Thanks to the fact that we have a very good economic situation - issues related to the sealing of tax systems - we have a chance to implement this programme so that others can benefit from it. The delay in the implementation of this law resulting from fear of even introducing symbolic water charges caused Poland to be threatened by huge penalties and the loss of several billion zlotys of EU money. I will remind you that for water with a capacity of up to 5 m3 per day for agricultural purposes and for the supply of water for humans and animals, there are no charges. If we multiply it by 365 days a year, because it's averaged, that's 18 grand. m3 water - and there are no fees, the farmer pays for nothing. If it wants to use water for other purposes, then in the case of surface waters it pays 5 gr for 1 m3, in the case of groundwater - 10 gr for 1 m3, in the case of irrigation - 15 gr for 1 m3. But I would like to remind you that the collection of surface water for agricultural irrigation is exempt from any charges, also there are no fixed charges. The rates that are currently in force are lower than in the past years, so these are absolutely symbolic rates for farmers who will decide to use groundwater. It's a drought case. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-04-sejm-83-2.u85	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Thank you very much for being able to answer the Members' questions. As far as the clean air programme is concerned, on the basis of the information we have from the national environmental fund, we can say that the programme is developing. As we know, the architecture of this program is based on this institution, but also on voivodship environmental funds. At this point, we have nearly 64,000 complexes. applications for PLN 1.5 billion - that is a significant amount - of which the value of the contracts currently signed amounts to around PLN 300 million. Thus, the number of contracts signed and their value are rapidly increasing. This is, of course, a natural process linked to the fact that the programme has an increasing interest on the part of potential beneficiaries. In response to the questions made by Mr and your fellow Members in the letter, I would like to draw attention to the fact that we are dealing with a joint effort here at the level of expert consultations between our government and the European Commission, and with regard to the Catching-up Regions initiative, the main role here is played by the Minister of Infrastructure, I am sorry, Minister for Investment and Development, Minister Kwieciński. It is such a point of contact - it talks with the European Commission. He is also the person who is discussing the future of the allocation of European funds to Poland for the next phase of funding. In a natural way, we are still ahead of the final decision on the size of this allocation. On the other hand, our discussion is taking place from a perspective where we are trying to deal with the whole issue of air quality with one umbrella undertaking. We have a clear demand on our side, a clearly defined goal - a programme for PLN 100 billion. On the other hand, we will now - together with the European Commission, but above all in the context of the judgments on the next financial perspective - determine the level of European funding for this programme. When it comes to optimization of the programme, of course we do this work. In a natural way, it is an undertaking which has a very fundamental, civilizational meaning for our country. It is about combating smog, thermomodernisation, reducing emissions from the municipal and real estate sector by 50% in the 2030 perspective. This is the powerful potential of 3 million homes. We are therefore also considering this and taking advantage of all possible opportunities to discuss how this programme can be optimized. This also applies to the distribution of resources. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-04-sejm-83-2.u99	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, (PL) Mr President, Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, The proceedings before the Court of Justice of the European Union referred to in the Member's statement are now at a final stage. The preliminary request in this case was submitted by the District Court in Warsaw on 16 April Ub. r. 21 August Ub. The Polish Government submitted written comments on this subject on the basis of the assumptions adopted by the Committee for European Affairs on 17 August. The written stage of the procedure was completed in November ub. and the Court decided not to hold a hearing in this case. On 14 May this year. The Advocate General delivered an opinion on the matter. We are now waiting only for a judgment to be delivered in the coming weeks, and a specific date is not yet specified. Therefore, due to the stage of proceedings, it is no longer possible to submit additional, complementary written comments, as the Polish Bank Association claims. It should also be made clear that the banking environment was represented in the proceedings before the Court of Justice, since one of the private banks is involved in this procedure, which made very extensive written comments in the proceedings. The banking environment therefore had the full opportunity to submit its arguments and arguments to the Court and to make use of this possibility. As regards the government's position, its aim was primarily to protect the rights of the weaker party to foreign exchange loans, i.e. consumers. These are the rights arising from Directive 93/13 of 5 April 1993 on unfair terms in consumer contracts. I would like to remind you that the District Court in Warsaw, which asked the Court of Justice, is deciding the matters of consumers who have taken credit in Swiss francs. In the loan agreement, the bank entered into an unauthorised contractual arrangement concerning the method of calculating the franc rate. The Bank stated that it was in its table that the rate at which the sum of the capital to be repaid and the amount of the instalment of the loan drawn up in francs, which the consumer must pay in zlotys, were to be calculated and clearly stated. Nor is there any doubt in the light of the case law of the Court of Justice of the European Union that such proceedings are not allowed within the meaning of the directive I have mentioned and consequently do not bind the consumer. The legal problem is how to continue to repay the credit after the removal of such illegal contractual provision. There is no provision in the credit agreement that would indicate how to calculate the rate of the franc. In these circumstances, consumers are either asking for an alternative or a void contract, which requires immediate payment to the bank of the sum that the bank has lent to the consumer, or further repayment of the credit, but in gold, of the sum that the consumer actually received from the bank, while maintaining a lower interest rate covering the margin and the LIBOR rate for the Swiss franc. Since both options are unfavourable to the bank, the bank would like to maintain the validity of the agreement when replacing the provision prohibited by the exchange rate of franc announced by the National Bank of Poland. Consequently, the district court asked whether the Unfair Contractual Conditions Directive would allow the replacement of a provision not authorised on the basis of the franc rate, established on the basis of general clauses, resulting from the Civil Code, i.e. based on the principles of social relations or customs. This, in the court's view, would allow the provision not permitted to be replaced by a rate determined by another indicator, e.g. published by the National Bank of Poland. Currently there are several thousand similar cases in Poland which are suspended pending the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union. The Polish Government presented to the Court the position in which it was pointed out that if the bank infringed the law and put in credit agreements provisions contrary to Directive 93/13 on unfair terms which I mentioned, such provisions cannot bind the consumer. The government pointed out that filling the gaps in the contract on the basis of the provisions of national law which contain general clauses would in no case be possible if the result of the recognition of certain contractual provisions as unfair would benefit the consumer from the collapse of the entire contract. An assessment of the consequences of the failure of the entire contract shall be made by the consumer after having been instructed by the court of all these consequences. The assessment expressed by the consumer should be taken into account by the court when deciding on the effects of the decision as inadmissible. What may be the effect of the Court's judgment? Banks which concluded credit agreements in Swiss francs in accordance with applicable law, i.e. they do not include unfair clauses in these agreements, they do not have to fear any adverse effects. On the other hand, banks which have taken advantage of their advantage over the consumer and introduced unfair clauses into credit agreements must face the consequences of EU law, just as any trader who carries out similar practices. EU law and the case-law of the Court of Justice very clearly and unambiguously protect the weaker side of credit agreements, which are borrowers. The position of the Polish Government presented in the proceedings before the Court is entirely based on this directive and the Court's case-law to date. However, so far the case law of the Polish courts in consumer matters has not been uniform. It has not always taken into account the requirements of EU law and has not always effectively protected the weaker side of credit agreements. We therefore hope that the Court's judgment in this case will contribute to the fact that the requirements arising from the Directive and the case-law of the Court of Justice of the European Union will be popularized both among the Polish courts and in the banking sector and will prevent banks from applying illegal contractual provisions in the future. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-04-sejm-83-2.u170	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation, I have the honour to present a report to the committee on the Government's draft law amending the Law on Maritime Safety, print No. 3540 and 3563. The Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation at its meeting on 3 July 2019 considered a draft law implementing two EU directives into the national legal order. The draft, after consideration by the committee, with one legislative amendment proposed by the Legislative Office concerning the article. Point 1 (1) was adopted with no votes against. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The implementation of the national legal order requires two passenger ship safety directives: the first is Directive 2017/2109 concerning the registration of persons travelling on board passenger ships, the second is Directive 2017/2110 concerning an inspection system for the safe operation of ro-ro passenger ships and high-speed passenger craft engaged in regular shipping. For this reason, the Minister of Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation prepared a draft law amending the Law on Maritime Safety containing regulations implementing the provisions of both Directives mentioned in Polish law. Key for passenger ship safety solutions contained in the draft Law amending the Maritime Safety Act concern inter alia. ensuring immediate access to the data of persons travelling on passenger ships by sea rescue services, which is extremely important in the case of the search and rescue operations of individual passengers or crew members of those ships, as well as in the case of the operation of so-called. mass rescue operations involving the entire passenger ship and all persons on board. The regulations on the system of inspection of ro-ro passenger ships and high-speed passenger craft operating on regular lines are also essential for passenger ships' safety. In addition to increasing the safety of passengers, the revised rules will also have a positive impact on the functioning of the maritime passenger transport sector, including: by abolishing overlapping requirements and harmonising requirements for passenger ships irrespective of their nationality. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! As rapporteur, I recommend the adoption of this draft law on the amendment of the Maritime Safety Act. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-04-sejm-83-2.u172	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft law amending the Maritime Safety Act contains a number of legal changes aimed at improving safety in Polish maritime areas. First of all, the proposed law contains provisions implementing two directives concerning the safety of passenger ships in the Polish legal order. The first Directive 2017/2109 concerns the registration of persons travelling on board passenger ships, and Directive 2017/2110 concerns an inspection system for the safe operation of ro-ro passenger ships and high-speed passenger craft engaged in regular shipping. The directives in place are clarifying the area of requirements for passenger ships, which in some cases have doubled and have been an unnecessary burden on ship owners of the passenger fleet. In addition, the directives put in place include solutions to improve search and rescue activities at sea, including: to ensure immediate access to the data of persons travelling on passenger ships by sea rescue services. The direction of change in European law, designated among others by the need to continuously improve the procedures for the operation of marine rescue services, it has also been reflected in the amendments I would like to make. These are four amendments, and the proposed amendments concern the functioning of the Maritime Search and Rescue Service units, the service set up to carry out search and rescue tasks at sea, the fight against marine hazards and pollution and other maritime safety-related tasks. The first amendment shall be made at the request of the shore workers of the rescue stations and the Marine Rescue Centre of the MSPiR Coordination Centre. The change will enable 24-hour on-calls to be carried out in these units, currently on duty for 12 hours, during which rescuers, inspectors remain alert to rescue, conduct radio surveillance and carry out tasks related to the operational readiness of the MSpiR's forces and resources. The same work system has been in place for a long time, among others. in the fire department or in the emergency services, i.e. in formations with similar specific tasks. This solution works in practice, meets the needs of workers, and gives employers the necessary flexibility in planning work and rest of rescue personnel. The second proposed amendment will improve the activities of the Maritime Search and Rescue Service and avoid absurd situations such as: leaving the ship shall have the grace of the element after the evacuation of its crew or of leaving the rescue ship in the so-called "saver" for hours. assist in a damaged unit in the event of a threat to the life of its crew as a result of a change in the weather or the development of a failure. 3. The amendment also provides for amendments to the Act, but given that the MSPiR has been set up to carry out a free rescue of human life and health and the protection of the marine environment, while marine rescue within the meaning of the Act The Maritime Code is a paid activity, it is necessary to regulate in the Law on Maritime Safety the principles of reimbursement to the budget of the State of the costs of service activities going beyond its original statutory tasks. 4. The amendment concerns the amendment to the wording of the Article. 8 draft law setting the date of entry into force of the Law amending the Law on Maritime Safety. In line with the proposed new wording of the Article. 8 the draft amendments concerning the maritime functioning of the Search and Rescue Service will enter into force within 14 days of the date of publication of the Act. Ladies and gentlemen! The proposed legal changes aim to improve the safety of all shipping professionals in Polish maritime areas, among others. by harmonising passenger ship requirements as well as improving the functioning of the Maritime Search and Rescue Service. In view of the above, on behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I request that this draft be adopted together with amendments not only by the committee but later by Parliament. This draft law aims to implement EU directives, so-called. a package of passenger directives aimed at simplifying and clarifying the existing EU legal framework for passenger ship safety. The revised regulations aim to meet the objective of the package through, among others, the aim of the package. the harmonisation of the rules for inspections carried out on ro-ro passenger ships and on high-speed passenger ships carrying more than 12 passengers on regular lines. The amendment foresees that inspections will take place twice a year, at an interval of not less than 4 months and not more than 8 months, which, according to the applicants, will provide passengers with a high level of safety while not interfering with the level or quality of the service or the fulfilment of the needs involved. What is equally important, according to the provisions in question, information on the number and identity of persons on board the ship will be transmitted directly to the electronic platform, which is the national single contact point, which involves the elimination of the requirement for shipowners to maintain passenger information registration systems. This is not only a reduction in bureaucratic burdens, but also, above all, by ensuring proper and timely transmission of information, an improvement in order to carry out possible rescue and search operations more effectively. As the designers point out, in practice, the single contact point is functioning but has not yet had a statutory mandate. The correct regulation of this issue is therefore, in my opinion, a good solution. It is also important to me that the amendment also includes provisions to protect shipowners from unjustified detention or delay of the ship in the port and, in the event of such a situation, provisions relating to compensation under the provisions of the Civil Code. I am convinced that this project will serve to increase safety on passenger ships. The Free and Solidarni Circle supports this project in its entirety.

If I do not hear opposition, I will think that the Sejm has accepted the offer. I can't hear you objecting. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-04-sejm-83-2.u204	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report from the Health Committee on the Government's draft law amending the Act on Nursing and Midwives' Competitions, prints No. 3520 and 3578. The first reading of the draft law took place at a committee meeting yesterday. The proposed regulation introduces a change in enabling people to take up training in the following fields: nursing and midwifery, as well as in the first degree studies conducted in a permanent form. At the same time, the draft law introduces a regulation on the possibility of receiving paid training leave for nurses and midwives up to 6 days per year, e.g. to participate in trainings, scientific conferences and scientific courses. According to the law in force, it is not possible to conduct first-cycle education in the following fields: nursing and midwifery in a non-stationary form, which in turn is possible in a uniform master's degree in medical and dental sciences. Education standards for courses: Nursing and obstetrics are defined, among others. duration of studies, minimum number of hours of classes and practices, as well as detailed learning outcomes, regardless of the form of study. Therefore, the proposed amendment ensures that the programme content of the first degree courses: nursing and obstetrics conducted in a permanent form and the number of hours in these studies will be the same as in the case of studies conducted in a stationary form. First-cycle education in the following fields: nursing and obstetrics conducted in a permanent form will be able to take up, first of all, working persons who wish to acquire new professional qualifications and to take up a job as a nurse or midwife. At present, there is an interest in education: nursing also by persons performing other medical professions, e.g. by paramedics or paramedics. This change will contribute to the implementation of one of the priorities adopted in the strategic and implementation document. National strategic framework. Policy paper for health protection 2014-2020, including the operational objective of supporting the training system of medical personnel in the context of adapting resources to changing social needs, and the Strategy for the Development of Nursing and Obstetrics in Poland of 2017. In 2015, the indicator on the number of nurses employed per thousand The population of the Republic of Poland was 5.2, while the average rate for the number of nurses employed was 1 000. The population in OECD countries was 9. In line with the strategy, one of the priorities is to take action to increase the number of candidates for nursing and midwifery, taking into account the quality of education. The priority is also to aim for an indicator on the number of nurses in the Republic of Poland to reach the level of the average index with the OECD, that was 9.4 for 2016, over a period of 15 years. This will allow, in view of the need for health care, an increase in the number of trained nurses and midwives due to the need to fill the generation gap that we are currently facing. The second major amendment contained in the draft law is the introduction of the possibility for a nurse or midwife to take part in training leave in order to constantly update knowledge and improve professional qualifications in forms other than those previously available, i.e. Specialized training, qualification course, specialized course and training course, as well as post-graduate training without referral, at the employer's request and with the employer's consent, up to 6 working days per year, paid in accordance with the rules applicable to the calculation of the remuneration for vacation leave. The length of training leave for nurses and midwives will be determined by the employer depending on the duration of the various forms of professional training. The draft law is one of the elements to improve the future situation regarding the number of nurses and midwives in employment. This objective is served by the government's efforts to increase the number of universities in which it is trained in the following fields: nursing and midwifery, increasing the number of students admitted, increasing the funding for education, improving the pay conditions, making the profession of nurse and midwife attractive. During the process of the bill, 3 amendments were notified and accepted. The whole draft, together with the amendments, was adopted by the committee with no objection. Therefore, on behalf of the committee, I request the support of the High House for the bill. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-04-sejm-83-2.u210	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! As is currently apparent from the requirements laid down in the Regulation of the Minister for Science and Higher Education of 9 May 2012, students who have decided to study on a fixed basis must take part in 1100 hours of practical activities and have 1200 hours of apprenticeships. These requirements can be very difficult to meet in the case of non-recurrent students who work on a daily basis. The next step may therefore be to amend the regulation governing this number of hours, reducing it to a more realistic level for absentee students. However, this solution will lead to a reduction in the level of education of our nurses, and we want patients to have the best possible care. Ladies and gentlemen! It's like the lens depicts the methods of governance in today's Poland: we promise, and others will pay. This situation is so dangerous that the equivalent for this leave is not included in any financial plans, and in the case of large centres these costs may be significant. This will deepen the financial crisis of many institutions. In conclusion, the projector wants to address the shortage of nurses and midwives only by seemingly increasing access to education in these areas or by reducing the level of education as a result of a reduction in the number of hours of practical activities. The costs of paid training leave to nurses and midwives under these provisions shall be transferred to hospital directors. Such management of health funds will soon result in the bankruptcy of many hospitals, the closure of further branches, which will significantly reduce access to health protection for all patients, including us and our loved ones. If, in the middle of the accounting year, we introduce rules that are costly for many institutions, it is a matter of fairness to fund them in their entirety, and only then to start negotiations with institutions to co-finance these ideas. Despite my sincere support for the nurse's demands, while appreciating their work and commitment, which I myself observe in my ward, I cannot accept the adoption of a cost-bearing project by the party which did not participate in the discussion of these solutions, nor is it a party to the agreement concluded on 9 July 2018 which resulted in this law. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-04-sejm-83-2.u252	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the parliamentary draft law amending the Energy Law Act. The amendments proposed in this draft law complement and clarify the provisions we find in the Energy Law in the Act of 10 April 1997. The obligations of the transmission system operator, the distribution system operator and the connected gas system operator as well as the powers of the President of the Energy Regulatory Office to shape the relationship between the owner of the gas transmission network and the operator of the gas transmission system will be specifically clarified. In our section of the Jamal gas pipeline we have the following situation: the owner of this section of the gas pipeline is EuRoPol GAZ, whose significant co-ownership is Russian Gazprom. The operator of the Polish part of Jamał is Gaz-System - here was appointed by the President of the Energy Regulatory Office until 2025. The current agreement on the delegation of obligations to the operator of the Jamal pipeline, which was concluded by Gaz-System and EuRoPol GAZ, which also regulates the mutual relations between companies, expires at the end of this year, on 31 December. It should also be noted that the content of the agreement currently in force does not allow Gaz-System to perform all the functions of the operator of the jamal pipeline, inter alia. with regard to the handling of gas pressing plants or the operation of the IT system, and thus is incompatible with the Polish interest and European law. Therefore, there is a serious risk that the signing of a new trust agreement, but one which will safeguard Polish interests and be compatible with European law, will be blocked by the Russian shareholder of EuRoPol Gazu, that is, Gazprom, and thus would not have any trust agreement since 1 January 2020, and Poland would have even less influence over control of the jamal pipeline in our territory. The bill that the High House will adopt, which we strongly urge as Law and Justice, is faced with this threat. It will oblige the parties to conclude the relevant agreement and, at the same time, it is very important, equips the President of the Energy Regulatory Office to determine the terms and conditions of the new delegation agreement by way of a decision which would not have been concluded between Gaz-System and EuRoPol Gaz 30 days before the expiry of the current agreement. In addition, the draft contains provisions allowing enforcement of the decision under the rules on enforcement in the administration. I stress that the project in no way impedes the transit of gas by the jamal gas pipeline, nor does it affect in any way the contract for the supply of natural gas to Poland concluded between PGNiG and Gazprom, i.e. by Gazprom, in force until the end of 2022. The project, although it affects very narrow matter, will have very serious consequences, because it will increase the energy security of Poland very much. And this is a priority of Law and Justice, strengthening the energy security of Poland. The less Russian influence on the gas system of Poland, the better. Of course, law and justice support this project and I encourage the entire High House to do so. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-04-sejm-83-2.u253	Thank you, sir. I invite Mr Paweł Bańkowski from the Civic Platform - Civic Coalition Club to present his speech on behalf of this club.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-04-sejm-83-2.u254	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Civic Platform - Civic Coalition Parliamentary Club, I have the honour to present the position of the club in relation to the report of the Energy and Treasury Commission on the draft law amending the Energy Law Act, print No 3497 and 3501. The bill entered the Sejm on 7 June 2019. On 10 June, it was sent to the first reading in the Committee on Energy and the Treasury. The project was examined at meetings on 12 June and 4 July. The amendments proposed in the draft law are intended to supplement and clarify the provisions of the Act of 10 April 1997. Energy law concerning the obligations of the transmission system operator, the distribution system operator and the connected gas system operator, as well as the powers of the national regulatory authority, i.e. the President of the Energy Regulatory Office in relation to the relationship between the owner of the gas transmission network and the operator of the gas transmission system or the operator of the connected gas system. The draft contains proposals for amendments to the Article. 9c, 9h and Article. Article 23 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2th Energy Law Act. Art. 9c determines the obligations of network operators. The proposed amendment to this provision consists in the addition of paragraph 1. 6b, which is to provide that, in the event of delegation of responsibility to the gas transmission system operator, gas distribution operator or gas connected system operator, that operator in its area of operation shall be responsible for issuing the terms of connection and for concluding and implementing the connection agreement. Art. The 9h of the Act lays down the rules for entrusting the transmission system operator with its duties. The amendment of this provision consists in the introduction of provisions relating to a situation where the President of the Energy Regulatory Office designates an energy undertaking as a gas transmission system operator on a specific transmission network for a longer period than the duration of the contract entrusting the transmission system operator with the use of that network. In such a situation, as proposed by the applicants, the transmission network owner would be obliged to conclude an agreement with the railway operator entrusting the transmission system operator with carrying out its duties. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Article 23 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 of the Act - it defines the scope of the President of the Energy Regulatory Office - applicants propose amendments to it, which are a consequence of changes in the article. 9h bill. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 2 of the draft law contains transitional provisions. The proposed law is to enter into force on the day following its publication in the Official Journal. With regard to the proposed amendments, it should be noted that the decision of the President of the URE to designate the gas transmission network operator under the current provisions of the Energy Law does not fully regulate the rights and obligations of the electricity undertaking and the owner of the network related to the operation of the transmission network operator by that undertaking. It is therefore necessary to conclude an agreement between the owner of the network and the energy company. The draft law also introduces the possibility for the President of the Energy Regulatory Office to issue a provision which, pending the final conclusion of the procedure for the adoption of a decision replacing the contract entrusting the transmission system operator with the obligations of the gas transmission system operator, will specify the conditions for entrusting the transmission system operator's duties to ensure continuity in the performance of the operator's functions. The transitional provisions provide that contracts entrusting the performance of the obligations of the gas transmission system operator concluded between the owner of the gas transmission network forming part of the transmission system, which on 3 September 2009 belonged to a vertically integrated undertaking and an energy undertaking licensed to carry out business activities in the field of the transport of gaseous fuels, before the date of entry into force of this Act until the expiry of those contracts, shall apply the provisions of the Energy Law as it stood. In view of the nature of the regulation in question and the urgent need to supplement the provisions of the Energy Law, applicants propose that the Act enter into force on the day following its publication in the Official Journal of the Law. This calls for the security of the gas transmission system and the possibility of using it. The draft law does not entail a burden on the state budget or budgets of local government units, does not provide for the issuing of implementing acts, is compatible with European Union law, does not affect the activities of micro-entrepreneurs and medium-sized enterprises, nor has it been consulted. During the examination of the draft law, seven amendments were tabled, which are based on comments submitted by the Energy Regulatory Office and are editorial and legislative in nature. [[♪ The bell ♪]] In conclusion, I would like to inform the High House that the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club - the Civic Coalition will vote in favour of this law. Thank you.

At that time, the Members of the PiS, Members of the parliamentary majority, completely unnecessarily rejected the amendments. The result of this parliamentary debate last year and this discussion on the history of Upper Silesia is an attempt to correct the error that actually occurred in the organization of these governmental celebrations, a mistake which is directly due to the failure to take into account the specifics of Silesian history in the celebration of the 100th anniversary of regaining independence. And the first sign that you want to withdraw from this mistake was the amendment of the resolution of the Council of Ministers creating the "Independant" programme. Such a change took place after this parliamentary debate in August 2018. In fact, it extended the application of the programme to 2022 to include the 100th anniversary of Silesia's accession to Poland. The bill we are debating today is another step in order to actually give away this historic justice. It seems that someone who had previously prepared the governmental celebrations of the century of regaining independence was in such a misconception, which unfortunately is quite common in Poland, that Silesia was joined to the Second Republic immediately after the Third Silesian Uprising in 1921. It is worth reminding that this was not the case, because historical truth is simply different. Decisions concerning the future of Silesia took place later, and in June 1922, Allied troops were stationed in its territory. Only on June 20, 1922, the Polish Army entered the Szopinice. Moreover, then the construction of the Polish administration began in Silesia. The Polish Army has been very solemn and very colourful in many cities for the following days. We remember these events in Silesia, often try to recreate them. Perhaps the Minister does not know that the greeting of Polish troops often looked so that such special reception gates were set up in Szopinice, in Katowice, in Chorzów, in many towns. This is something we remember. We often try to recreate these installations on the occasion of various kind of round anniversaries about these events. We would like to be remembered in Poland, as we remember in Silesia, e.g. about the Wielkopolska Uprising, about other important anniversaries from the history of Poland. We would also like to remember our Silesian history in Poland, which is slightly different from the history of other regions. Gentlemen of Ministers! Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to point out that the amendment to the Rules of Procedure consists in the extension of the Rule. 175 by the addition of paragraph 1. The following 8 shall be added: Until the Member has lodged an appeal, the Marshal of the Sejm may waive his decision to exclude the Member from the sitting of the Sejm. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The institution for excluding a Member from a Sejm meeting shall be governed by Article. Point (c) of the first subparagraph is replaced by the following: 5, 6 and 7 Rules of Procedure of the Sejm. According to Art. Point (c) of the first subparagraph is replaced by the following: 5 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, where a Member, after twice referred to the Sejm, continues to prevent proceedings from being held, the Marshal of the Sejm shall have the right to decide to exclude a Member from the Sejm meeting. Member excluded from the meeting of the Sejm is required immediately leave the sitting room, and if he does not, the Marshal of the Sejm shall manage the adjournment. The exclusion from the meeting of the Sejm is one of the procedural powers of the Marshal or Vice-Marshal of the Sejm in respect of Members. According to Art. Point (c) of the first subparagraph is replaced by the following: 6 Rules of Procedure of the Sejm excluded from the Sejm may appeal against the decision to exclude it from the Sejm Bureau, which shall settle the matter without delay after consulting the Rules of Procedure, Parliaments and Immunities Committee. Until the final settlement of the case, the Member shall not take part in the sitting. The Sejm shall inform the Sejm immediately of the manner in which the appeal is to be taken, but no later than at the next meeting. The decision of the Sejm Bureau shall be final. Currently, the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm do not provide for the possibility for the marshal or vice-marshal to withdraw from the decision to exclude a Member. However, it seems that the excluding Member from the meeting of the Sejm should be able to withdraw from his decision. That's what purposeful considerations speak for. In some cases, maintaining the decision to exclude a Member from the meeting of the Sejm may prove less effective than withdrawing from that decision for the purpose of bringing order to the session of the Sejm. For sometimes, from the point of view of the aim of this institution, concerted action may be more effective, which will result in the withdrawal of the Marshal or Vice-Marshal from the decision to exclude the Member and, on the other hand, in the cessation of the conduct of the Member preventing the proceedings from taking place. The draft foresees that the Marshal of the Sejm will be able to waive his decision to exclude a Member from the Sejm's meeting until the Member has lodged an appeal. The decision to exclude a Member from the meeting will only be open to the Marshal or Vice-Marshal of the Sejm, who originally took this decision. Only in such a case shall this be without prejudice to the provisions providing for an appeal procedure against the decision to exclude a Member, i.e. Article. Point (c) of the first subparagraph is replaced by the following: 6 Rules of Procedure of the Sejm. Secondly, it seems that too far-reaching interference with the provisions of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm concerning the appeal procedure would be allowed to withdraw the decision to exclude from the sitting after the Member has lodged an appeal with the Sejm Bureau. In such a situation, i.e. It seems that the Bureau will have the exclusive opportunity to raise the decision to exclude a Member. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

We proceed with the examination of item 31 of the agenda: Report on the operation of the Supreme Audit Chamber in 2018 (Print No 3366) together with the opinion of the Commission for State Control (Print 3515). ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-04-sejm-83-2.u300	Mrs. Marshal! Mr Fellowships and Mr Fellow Members, This year's report on the activities of the Supreme Audit Chamber is particularly timely. In February this year we celebrated the 100th anniversary of the Supreme Auditorium. So let me start with a fundamental reflection on NIK. The creators of the Supreme Audit Authority in both II and III of the Republic were convinced that credible budgetary control and control of performance of tasks could only be carried out by a government-independent audit body whose members of the college and controllers would be created with far-reaching guarantees so that they were not afraid to formulate critical assessments, they were not concerned about any repercussions related to it. Therefore, in describing the actions of the Supreme Audit Chamber of both the first, from 1921, prepared under the direction of Józef Higersberger and the latter, from 1994, prepared under the direction of Prof. Lech Kaczyński, the independence of the Supreme Audit Chamber, its collaborators, all controllers, regardless of their positions were guaranteed. This report is also unique from my point of view for one more reason, because it is the last one I present. At the end of August this year, I end my term as President of the Supreme Audit Chamber. Ladies and gentlemen! But it is not only the Supreme Chamber of Control law that regulates the work of this authority. Also the constitution, the most important legal act and the foundation of the legal order of the Republic of Poland, in art. 202 states that the Supreme Audit Chamber is subject only to the Sejm. However, as all commentators have said, this is not the case in any way of making control arrangements or making evaluations. In this area, the Chamber is fully independent and independent, as confirmed by the judgment of the Constitutional Court of December 1998. Without independent state control, it would be difficult to build a democratic rule of law. This is clearly stated by the declaration from Lima adopted by the International Organization of Supreme Audit Authorities Congress, but it is also highlighted in its documents by the United Nations. High Izbo! It is from this independence of the Supreme Audit Chamber, and especially its controllers, regardless of the position taken, from the inspector to the counsellor or the director, that the credibility of our audit reports and their importance to the citizens are in great faith. They see that we act independently of the fact that our reports are reliable and objective. When I came to work at NIK 6 years ago, the work of the Chamber was well assessed by about 1/3 Poles. Today, the latest study shows that nearly 50% of adult Poles - which is important, regardless of political sympathy - thus evaluates the work of NIK as good or very good. I stress this number of people who positively assess the work of the Chamber, as it has increased in recent years from year to year. Today, NIK is rated as one of the best working institutions in our country. This is also appreciated by people from different political backgrounds. Former Prime Minister Jerzy Buzek has repeatedly stressed the extraordinary reliability and professionalism of NIK controllers or involvement in the work of the Board's management. The current President Andrzej Duda, who hosted the presidents of the control bodies of the countries of the European Union a few days ago, i.e. the so-called. The Contact Committee, which I presided over, stressed that I would like to quote that the Supreme Audit Chamber in Poland has an extraordinary appreciation, a great estimate and great respect, and NIK and its controllers comply with the highest standards. NIK reports are also highly appreciated by media representatives, by external and industry experts both in Poland and abroad. They stress the high quality of the findings, evaluations and conclusions presented by the Chamber and their importance for the state recovery process. It is also very important to assess the contemporary condition of the Chamber that the Chamber enjoys in the international arena. NIK is currently on the board of EUROSAI, the European Organisation of Supreme Audit Authorities, where I am Vice-President, and on the board of the International Organisation of Supreme Audit Authorities, INTOSAI. It is not only an expression of trust in our organization, but also an expression of appreciation for our activity. Nearly every year, NIK is selected by various international groups to help other countries with European aspirations, or to audit the most prestigious international institutions and organisations such as the European Nuclear Research Organisation, the Council of Europe or the Organisation for Economic Cooperation and Development, the OECD. When pointing to the objective and external sources of NIK's assessment in the context of the 2018 report, we cannot overlook yet another very important source: the opinions expressed on NIK by individual Sejm committees, with which controllers from all departments and delegations cooperate on a daily basis. Members of these committees they know best the findings contained in our reports, value the attached system analyses or share the conclusions. All the Sejm committees, presenting their opinions to the Commission for State Control, assessed the work of the Supreme Audit Chamber positive or unnoticed. Thank you very much for this objectivity of the Parliamentary Committees. The good external evaluations of NIK from many sources, including from the audit auditor commissioned by the NIK Sejm, show that the Chamber is functioning properly, that its audit staff and management are well prepared to carry out tasks, and that the methods and standards adopted for carrying out audits are well suited to the challenges facing the Chamber. By building our quality assurance system, we used international standards of state control, in particular INTOSAI quality control standards, an international organisation that brings together all control authorities in the world. High Seym! We have provided you with all the details in a comprehensive document, a report on the House's activities. I would like to draw attention to the fact that, due to the limited time frame for the appearance of the Seym, I will only mention the most important figures depicting the work of the House and mention the absolutely few key problems and some selected audits. You will find the rest in a nearly 500-page report on our activities. This Decision shall enter The House carried out 2325 inspections, i.e. 129 more than in the previous year. We have included these controls - because in some entities we have been many times - over 1800 entities. The areas relevant to citizens and the functioning of the State have been examined in the framework of 111 planned controls. This figure also includes a control of the implementation of the State budget, which is treated as one, but in fact consists of 285 checks carried out in 267 units. By creating a work plan for last year, we also took advantage of the suggestions of the Sejm committees. In total, we have adopted almost 90% - wider or narrower - in this respect. the various applications that have affected you. In addition to scheduled inspections, NIK has also carried out 91 ad hoc checks, most often in response to signals coming to the House about serious irregularities. Complaints addressed to the House were an important source of inspiration for us to undertake ad hoc checks. In 2018, more than 4 000 arrived at the House. complaints and requests, of which almost 2,5% constitute complaints and requests submitted by parliamentarians and by Parliament's bodies. All complaints ordered by thematic groups have initiated many of our controls or have been forwarded to other competent public authorities. At the request of NIK, other state control bodies carried out almost 150 ad hoc inspections and made available to us the results of another 14 inspections. In 2018, we submitted 191 information to the Sejm on the results of the planned audits, including the implementation of the budget and certain ad hoc controls. The House's representatives attended a total of over 300 meetings of the Sejm committees. Last year, the House also formulated 140 de lege ferenda proposals, including 108, in the context of the need for legislative changes. The level of implementation of these proposals is, of course, around 10% during the first period. , but since the legislative process lasts longer, these results are obviously significantly higher. If we look at the level of implementation of these proposals, I can say that in recent years about 1/3 is already implemented, except for those that are under way. It should also be mentioned that in 2018, in addition to the 140 de lege ferenda proposals contained in the information and statements made in the speeches, we made 4851 follow-up proposals, with more than 300 more applications than in 2017. More than 80% of these proposals have already been implemented or are being implemented. It can therefore be concluded, without exaggeration, that the controlled institutions benefit from most of our proposals. In this way, NIK, among others. it contributes to improving the functioning of the State each year. Ladies and gentlemen! Each year you also ask about the financial benefits obtained by the Treasury as a result of our checks. These amounts amount to nearly PLN 44 million. This is a measurable effect of our controls. However, as with the de lege ferenda proposals, sometimes the process of obtaining benefits for the State Treasury can be time-consuming. In August 2017, the European Commission issued a decision which ordered Poland to recover almost PLN 900 million together with interest from Autostrada Wielkopolska. All this started with our 2010 audit, when we found that the government had paid too much compensation to private operators for releasing trucks from tolls on motorways. In March 2018, the company therefore had to pay over PLN 1300 million for the deposit sums of the Minister of Finance. You see, it often happens that the financial effects of our work are spread over time. The same is true of reports of crimes or offences committed. Last year we put in about 100 and it was 17 more than last year. We have also submitted 58 notifications to the Ombudsman of public finance discipline in which we have disclosed 259 violations of financial discipline. High Izbo! Like I said, I'm not going to tell you about all the checks. I'm just gonna say a few. I think one of the most important controls last year was the audit of air protection measures against pollution. Our report on this topic was presented in September Ub. We found that within the European Union Poland is one of the countries with the worst air quality. We also assessed that the actions of the relevant ministries, but also of voivodship and municipal authorities, except in a few cases, were far from sufficient. Knowledge of the audit methodology for examining air quality improvements has resulted in the highest control authorities from several European countries being entrusted with the duties of an international control coordinator in this area, in which, as I said, several European countries participated. Ladies and gentlemen! In view of the impact of environmental protection on the lives and health of citizens, the Supreme Audit Chamber has also carried out an inspection of the maintenance and operation of water supply networks in cities. Another important area affecting citizens' lives is the organisation and functioning of the health system. We devoted a number of checks to this issue last year. We studied, among others. availability and waiting time for health benefits. Unfortunately, in the case of 30% of benefits, the waiting time in the doctor's queue was extended at the end of 2017 compared to 2016. Another audit concluded that the health care system for children and young people of school age, particularly as regards prevention, does not guarantee the full health safety of this age group, as evidenced by the increasing number of children and young people with health problems. The subject of health protection is such an important issue for NIK that we have prepared a comprehensive report, a kind of megainformation, based on the findings of more than 70 inspections of the House, of course including those of recent years, as well as on the opinions of doctors, directors, representatives of hospital founding bodies, health professionals, and [[♪ The bell ♪]] patient associations. Our report met with an excellent reception among external experts. It is also available to all those who want to learn about the most important health problems and recommendations of NIK. Ladies and gentlemen! For many years we have been dealing with problems related to tax collection, including VAT, in NIK. In 2018, we continued our checks in this area, appreciating the greater effectiveness of the tax system in preventing VAT fraud. While seeking proper public spending, we have also been interested in the issue of the management of State Treasury companies and the exercise of ownership supervision. Signals about irregularities were coming to us, among others. from the Parliamentary Committee. We audited the expenditure of the Treasury companies for sponsorship, media and advisory services. 20 companies examined by us spent more than PLN 1.5 billion on sponsorship activities and the purchase of advisory and media services. Among the most important irregularities we included spending money on sponsorship despite the negative financial result of the company or sponsoring projects that were not included in the spending plans. Ladies and gentlemen! In 2018 we also examined the functioning of the Polish Armed Group and the payment of checks in selected companies of the State Treasury. The reports are ready and will soon be in the Sejm. Some of our information about the results of the checks we send to the Sejm in the manner appropriate for classified information. That was the case, for example. when we were checking how Poland is prepared for terrorist threats. An important and interesting report on this matter was recently sent to the Sejm. Ladies and gentlemen! In conclusion, I can say that the main role of the NIK is to provide parliamentarians, representatives of the executive authority and, most importantly, to all citizens with reliable, in-depth and objective information on various important areas. Free citizens need to know how government and local government agendas perform their statutory tasks and how they spend public money. Today, 100 years after the establishment of the Supreme National Chamber, it seems that its importance is even greater. In an age of information noise and threats of deliberate manipulation and disinformation, it is difficult to imagine the existence of a democratic country without reliable, in-depth and objective information provided by an independent body responsible for State control. High Seym! I'm finishing my six-year term as CEO of NIK. I leave the House in very good condition, highly appreciated by public opinion, external experts, international institutions, journalists and parliamentarians, but above all I leave the House, where 1600 professionals, the best specialists in state control in Poland, are well-educated, well-prepared. I can say that regardless of the position you are taking, whether it is an inspector, a specialist, a counselor, or a director, you perform your duties with dedication, commitment and professionalism. Thank you for your service together. I can end with wishes directed to my successor, so that so far the Supreme Chamber of Control is synonymous with reliable, reliable, objective information that will serve all citizens and parliamentarians in the Polish Seym and the Polish Senate. The Supreme Audit Chamber focuses primarily on systemic irregularities, public administration activities and factors that threaten the proper achievement of the State's objectives and problems which are particularly serious for citizens. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-04-sejm-83-2.u344	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. President! I listened attentively to Mr Krzysztof Kwiatkowski's report on the activities of the Supreme Audit Chamber. The main objective of each NIK audit is to determine the actual situation in the area concerned, reliable documentation and evaluation. The activities of the local authorities shall be verified in terms of legality, economy and reliability. Non-public entities - in terms of legality and economy and state entities, e.g. State administrations - in terms of legality, economy, reliability and purpose. Inspectors shall, when verifying the legality of the activities audited, pay particular attention to whether they are carried out in accordance with the provisions of applicable law. The analysis of the controlled activity in terms of economy is intended to indicate whether the money was used cost-effectively and efficiently and the expenditure was proportional to the results obtained. As regards reliability, the inspectors verified whether the audited entities fulfilled their duties with due care, diligence and timely care. With regard to the purpose criterion, it is only applicable in the case of control of public entities. The purpose is to assess whether the activities carried out by the audited entity were for the purposes set for it by law. However, returning to the report, an important control of the NIK was the control of procedures applied to those wishing to adopt children. What the NIK particularly draws attention to is the lack of uniform state requirements and criteria applied during adoption, which makes practice significantly different in individual centres. The main reasons for the extended adoption procedure are the lack of children with a regulated legal situation, which prevents adoption, and the long-term waiting for training of candidates for parents. The average duration of the adoption procedure is 2 years. This refers to the period from the time of registration of parents in the database to the decision by the court to grant custody of the child. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-04-sejm-83-2.u346	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! This proposal implements the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 15 March 2017, specifically Article. Article 9 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2, by penalising the conduct of travelling to another Member State, as regards intentional conduct, in order to commit or contribute to the terrorist offence referred to in that Directive. Secondly, it is about participating in the activities of an organised terrorist group, knowing that such participation will contribute to the criminal activities of that group and the conduct of activities or training for terrorism, as referred to in Article. 7 and 8 directives. The implementation of the Directive will be carried out by amending the article that is currently envisaged to commit a terrorist offence. Then, in view of these issues, we propose that we remove the article from the provision. 259a in the territory of another country. So, by renouncing the article. 259a of the Penal Code by deleting the words "in the territory of another country' from it, we will thus be able to punish acts involving crossing the border, crossing the territory of another Member State for the purpose of committing a terrorist offence or an offence referred to in Article. 255a, i.e. dissemination of content likely to facilitate the commission of a crime, or Article. 258 § 2 or 4, i.e. organized group and criminal union. This will extend the scope of criminalisation to conduct involving crossing the Polish border, also in order to commit the above mentioned crimes in its territory. So much for the project. The autocorrection, on the other hand. In the first point, it strengthens the responsibilities of multi-religiousists. Council Framework Decision of 24 October 2008 on combating organised crime obliges our country to adapt its law towards adopting appropriate rules, sanctions and recognition as an aggravating circumstance for participation in an organised criminal group. I'm talking specifically about art. 3. In particular, it's an article. 3 (1) (a) 2 The Framework Decision requires the Member State to take the necessary measures to ensure that the fact that a criminal offence has been committed in an organised criminal group should be regarded as an aggravating circumstance. That's one thing. Secondly, the recommendations of the Commission Communication to the Council and the European Parliament of 2 June 2005 indicate that more severe penalties, appropriate to the offences committed by organised criminal groups, should be implemented. The lack of strict regulation in this respect, as regards the return to the crime of members of organised criminal groups, is an inappropriate, inadequate implementation of the provisions of Framework Decision 2008/841, as well as those recommendations. It is also worth mentioning in the third paragraph the United Nations Convention against International Organised Crime of 15 November 2000, which Article. Article 11 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 points to the need to tighten up penalties for members of organised criminal groups. In the meantime, ladies and gentlemen, in Polish law, multiple offenders, and therefore persons who are sentenced for the third time to imprisonment, can count on conviction within the lower limits of the sentence. The rule clearly indicates: above the lower limit of the statutory threat, above, so it can only be 1 month above. The proposal therefore provides that, in the face of such perpetrators, multi-religiousists, the court will have to impose a sentence of imprisonment ranging from a double of the lower statutory threat increased by half. Today it can only tighten it up, but it does not have to. Secondly, tightening the responsibilities of sexual offenders. Art. The 9th Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 13 December 2011 on combating sexual abuse and sexual exploitation of children and child pornography shows that the offences referred to therein should be severely punished. In particular, it's about sex offenders. Meanwhile, in Polish law, these perpetrators can count on the sentence of imprisonment even below the lower statutory threat through the so-called. exceptional relaxation of the penalty. Article 1 shall apply. 64 § 1, which indicates that they are punishable to the upper limit of the statutory threat increased by half. The draft provides that the court will have to impose a sentence of imprisonment ranging from the lower limit of the statutory threat increased by half to the upper limit of the statutory threat increased by half, both in the case of offences and crimes. Today, first of all, the court can only tighten the upper limit, and it does not have to do so, second, it does not concern crime at all, and thirdly, we have this situation today that the court can impose punishment within the lower limits, even below the lower limits. Thus, our current legislation does not implement this Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council. Thirdly, tightening the responsibility of perpetrators of crimes and crimes within organized criminal groups. Council Framework Decision of 24 October 2008, which I have already mentioned, on combating organised crime in Article. 3 (1) (a) 2 obliges Member States to treat as an aggravating circumstance the perpetrators in organised criminal groups. However, in our legislation, we have a solution that allows the court to punish such perpetrators within its lower limits. Art. 65, which refers to Article. 64 § 2, indicates the penalty: above the lower limit of the statutory threat, thus generally such a penalty can be imposed within the lower limits. There is a proposal that, against the perpetrators of such crimes, but also crimes, penalties should be increased accordingly. As far as vices are concerned, twice the lower risk of crimes, half the increase. Another change is the tightening of the responsibility of perpetrators in organised criminal groups, setting up and directing these criminal groups, Article. 258. This is an appropriate increase in sanctions, twice in each type of prohibited act indicated in those paragraphs. Finally, the self-correction proposal provides for compliance with European Union law on searches in prisons, as well as on telephone calls made by inmates in prisons. Art. Article 6 (6) (a) 3 of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union indicates that rights guaranteed by the European Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms and resulting from constitutional traditions common to Member States form part of Union law as general principles of law. In its judgment in Dejnek v Poland and Milka v Poland, the Court pointed out that there is no doubt that the search rules should be governed by the law. The requirement to undergo personal checks must be governed by the law, in accordance with the case law of the European Court of Human Rights, which is transposed into European Union law, that is, Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 3 treaty. The same decision was issued by our Constitutional Tribunal in its judgment of 14 December 2017, sygn. Act 17/14. Thus, the self-correction implements the standards of the case law of the European Court of Human Rights, fulfilling both the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union and our Constitutional Tribunal's requirements. And last but not least, ensuring security in the penitentiary units. Here, too, the mentioned article. Article 6 (6) (a) 3 of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union, referring to the law of the Council of Europe, requires us to refer to the European prison rules, which indicate in point 24.2 that communication and visits may be subject to the restriction and monitoring necessary for the continuation of investigations, the maintenance of order, security, the prevention of criminal offences and the protection of victims of crime. However, such restrictions, including certain restrictions imposed by the judicial authorities, may provide for minimum contact. And the amendment introduced in July 2015 allowed for contact without any restrictions. The adoption of this solution disorganized the internal order in penitentiary facilities, caused gigantic abuses by those deprived of freedom, forced the need to adapt the rules of operation of the penitentiary units to one of the elected powers of those deprived of freedom. In fact, the full implementation of the solutions adopted at that time has made it difficult and, in extreme cases, prevented other people who were deprived of freedom from exercising their powers. After all, if one person calls all the time, another person won't be able to make those calls. Even at school, a student can't make phone calls during class when he likes it, but in prison, the inmates have the right to a phone call at any nod and call their wives, lovers or lawyers. We're introducing new rules. The right to contact, of course, remains - the right to contact a lawyer, a lawyer once a week - and it must be clearly pointed out that, first of all, this must not affect the rights of others imprisoned by blocking their access to such calls, and secondly, it should not interfere with the order established in the prison. The adoption of the proposed solution will maintain the right of persons deprived of freedom to contact the defender, in justified cases even in unlimited quantities - Article. 8 § Article 2 and Article 2 217c § 1a of the proposed amendment. This will also allow full and secure implementation of the powers of other inmates. This will not limit absolutely other forms of contact between those deprived of freedom and their legal representatives. At the moment, there are more than 9 thousand in circulation. consents for persons temporarily arrested. It is estimated that in one unit there are about 127 approvals of this type. The head of such an entity shall in no way be able to organise or arrange such contacts with the outside world, which could give rise to the risk of abuse and the risk of the unlawful transfer of information of all kinds outside the penal facility. This could also threaten the victims of the crime and its witnesses, so ordering this system is absolutely necessary. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-04-sejm-83-2.u354	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats, I would like to present a position on the government proposal for a law amending the Penal Code, prints no. 3386 and 3386-A. However, it should be noted that most of the provisions of this Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council have already been implemented in the Polish legal system. As part of the self-correction accompanying the project, the authors propose a number of other amendments to the Penal Code. The draft proposes to give a new wording to the article. 155 of the Penal Code, which determines the penalty for manslaughter, in line with the demands of among others. The Chief Medical Council... [[Voice from the room: There is no such thing.]] ...and the Chief Chamber of Nurses and Obstetrics. Currently, manslaughter is punishable by imprisonment from 3 months to 5 years. The Penal Code provides for the same sanction, e.g. For shoplifting, forgery of school cards, and destruction of property. [[Mr President, this is not the project.]] Therefore, the authors of the project propose to place these borders between 6 months and 8 years of imprisonment. Such a solution will provide a wider possibility for sentences of imprisonment to be imposed in the extent that the sentence can be suspended on a conditional basis. The next amendment concerns the addition of a new wording to the article. 65 of the Criminal Code - new principles of punishment, criminal measures and measures to test the perpetrator against certain perpetrators who commit crimes in connection with circumstances indicating the organized and planned nature of criminal activity. This proposal is aimed at combating the most serious organised crime and aims to take appropriate measures towards this category of perpetrators to carry out the protective functions of criminal law. In turn, the amendments relating to the implementation of the new Chapter XXa in the Penal Code, the Control of convicted and provisionally arrested, require the execution of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal. The new chapter regulates the way in which convicted and temporarily arrested persons are dealt with. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In conclusion, the project should continue to be procedated. I also have a problem, it is what the previous speakers said, and it concerns this self-correction, which is extremely extensive in terms of the subject matter of regulation.
