ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-04-sejm-83-2.u386	Mr. Marshal! Prime Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to be the rapporteur during the process of dealing with this very important law ordering the situation of one of the most recognizable places in Poland and the world in connection with the Second World War - Westerplatte. The report of the Committee on Infrastructure in print No 3544 concerns the Parliamentary draft law on investments in the construction of the Westerplatte Museum and the War of 1939 - the branch of the World War II Museum in Gdańsk from print No 3454. The second reading took place on 3 July this year. During the second reading, a proposal was made to reject the project and four amendments were tabled. The project was again addressed to the Infrastructure Committee for the purpose of submitting a report. During the committee's work on 3 July, the proposal to reject the draft law in its entirety, together with the amendments, was negative. The Commission asks the High Sejm to adopt the attached bill. The project sets out the terms, conditions and manner of preparation and implementation of investments in the construction of the Westerplatte Museum and the 1939 War required for reasons of public interest. It shall also specify the competent authorities for the preparation and implementation of the investments referred to above and the sources of financing thereof. High Izbo! Westerplatte is an extremely important place for every Pole. It was there that the Germans invaded Poland, and soon after this event war activities covered almost the whole world. That's where our soldiers resisted the enemy. Westerplatte became a symbol of the heroic struggle for Poland during the first hours of the war. There are many examples of places in the world where a soldier's struggle, the death of a soldier in defence of his own homeland or other nations, is extremely worthy of commemoration. Thermopylae, Pearl Harbor or Normandy are for all places clearly associated with fighting, sacrificing battles for their homeland. Thousands of white crosses at the cemetery in Normandy are impressive. The place where the first defenders of the Polish land were killed has been kind of forgotten and underestimated for years. Although this place has recently been cleaned up, during the first reading of the project at the meeting of the Committee on Infrastructure on 18 June many invited guests confirmed the state of this place with their words and various photographs. A few days later, I found a lot of neglect on the peninsula. None other than the President of Gdańsk, referring to the proceedings of the committee, speaking about the outbreak of the Second World War, said the words of Germany, but said that 80 years ago the Second War began, which brought the regime and slavery. Today, the Sejm of the Republic of Poland adopted an amendment to the Act on the National Celebration of the 100th Anniversary of regaining independence by Poland, extending the period of commemoration of this epochous event in the history of our nation until 2022. As a Member from Upper Silesia, I thank the High House for this amendment, which will allow the celebration of the 100th anniversary of the return of Upper Silesia to its mother in 1922. The inhabitants of Upper Silesia want to solemnly celebrate their next important anniversaries, that is the 100th anniversary of the outbreak of the 1st, 2nd and 3rd Uprising and the 100th anniversary of the plebiscite, but above all - joining the part of Upper Silesia to the brightest of the Republic of Poland. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! This year marks the 100th anniversary of the outbreak of the First Silesian Uprising, the first in a series of three raids of the Upper Silesian people, which led to the fact that during the interwar period a significant part of Upper Silesia was located within the borders of the Republic of Poland. And the Silesian Uprising, which broke out on 16 August 1919 and lasted only until 24 August of that year, was the most spontaneous of the Silesian Uprisings. It was a reaction to repression by the German authorities of that time, but above all a manifestation of the desire of the Silesian people to join Upper Silesia to the reborn Polish country. Three more years had to wait for that dream to come true. There were still many sacrifices and donations of blood, so that in 1922 part of Upper Silesia would be within the borders of the Republic of Poland. How did it happen that the inhabitants of Upper Silesia, who had been outside Poland for centuries, kept this dream of living in a common country? The common tradition, common faith and common language, Catholic faith, which brought us together, Poles from the Republic of Poland, and after its fall - with Poles living in Austrian or Russian partitions, the tradition which referred to Catholic faith and referred to the Old Polish language, which still preserved many of these archaisms of Polish language. Now there are people and organizations who are trying to relativize the history of the Silesian Uprisings and rewrite it. They try to sacrifice our ancestors in opposition to Poland both in the interwar and contemporary period. Starting with the celebration of the 100th anniversary of subsequent uprisings, we must restore collective memory both among the inhabitants of Upper Silesia and residents of other regions of Poland the historical truth about events that took place in Upper Silesia, and about their impact on the shape of the Second Republic of Poland. We owe it to those who spilled their blood years ago to keep Upper Silesia within Poland, we owe it to all present inhabitants of Silesia and also to all Poles. Greater knowledge of the history, tradition and culture of this region will help to better understand the current situation. The first, but I hope, not the only strong accent of this dissemination of knowledge about Silesia can be the unveiling of the monument of Wojciech Korfanty in Warsaw from the funds of the state budget. I hope that in the next three years we will take many other initiatives that will commemorate these historical events and the people who created these events. Thank you from this place, Minister Sellin, who assured today that there will be financial resources to commemorate these very important events for our homeland and Silesia. It is certainly optimistic that these events will allow for historical education of the inhabitants of Silesia, especially here I mean young people and our homeland, which will allow - once again - for a better understanding of the tradition and history of this land. Thank you very much.

These genes are already known, there are a lot of them, therefore there are dozens of forms of this disease depending on what gene it is and what factor is missing, what protein the genetic deviant is about: keratin, pectin, laminine, collagen. Skin disease is often accompanied by deafness, blindness, atresia of the duodenum puppet or hypoplasia of other organs. The worst thing for those children who suffer from actually opening their eyes in the morning until closing them in the evening is skin damage, bursting blisters. Mothers must pierce or break through these blisters spontaneously. The only proper treatment in terms of care is, in fact, only symptomatic treatment, the use of modern, selected for a given type of dressing disease. These bandages are very expensive. In 2015, monthly treatment of the child cost 372 PLN, by the end of June of this year as many as 4 thousand, and now the cost in connection with the photo from the refund list from 1 July is 10 and 12 thousand. depending, of course, on how big the child is and how large the skin must be covered. There is a causal treatment, which is an experimental treatment for the time being. Namely Professor McGrath in Austria transplants stem cells with the missing gene, the already corrected one, and it offers a chance to heal. Similar trials are carried out in the United States. But at the moment, I, on behalf of these children, their parents, ladies and gentlemen... I know how a child suffers from cancer pain, it's temporary pain. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-04-sejm-83-2.u563	Thank you very much, Member. I invite you to make a statement by Mr Arkadiusz Mularczyk, Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-04-sejm-83-2.u564	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I wanted to make a statement on the 20th anniversary of Władysław Hasior's death. 20 years after Władysław Hasior's death, it is worth mentioning this one of the most outstanding Polish artists of the 20th century, and according to many - the forerunner of the world pop-art, to say a few words about his achievements. He was born on 14 May 1928 in Nowy Sącz. Between 1947 and 1952 he studied at the State High School of Plastic Techniques in Zakopane under Professor Antoni Kenar, and then in 1952 he studied at the Academy of Fine Arts in Warsaw under the supervision of Prof. Marian Wnuk. In 1959, he received a scholarship from the French Minister of Culture. He studied at Ossip Zadkine's sculptor's studio in Paris. Since 1957 he has exhibited individually and participated in collective exhibitions in Poland and abroad, among others. in Paris, Rome, Stockholm, Oslo, Montevideo, Säo Paulo, Helsinki, Vienna, Valencia, Copenhagen, Brussels and Moscow. During his studies, he engaged in youth work at the Zakopane State High School of Plastic Techniques and taught from now until 1968. Since 1959, he has participated in all the exhibitions of the Zakopane Artists District and in the actions of the Artists Grouped at the State High School of Plastic Techniques. He was the founder of the Pegasus Gallery, participated in the artistic life of the city, initiated many cultural events of a national character. He was the President of the Association of Polish Plastic Artists in Zakopane, as well as lectured at the State Higher School of Plastic Arts in Wrocław. He worked creatively all the time, exhibited his works throughout Poland and abroad. His works were represented at more than 80 individual exhibitions in Poland, he also gained great appreciation of critics around the world, and his exhibitions were organized by the most important cultural institutes in Europe and the world. Hasior was one of the most outstanding and original Polish contemporary artists. In his works, the material of which was various materials, ordinary objects took on new meanings. He created banners, imaginative portraits, original paintings containing a whole range of references to reality or artistic traditions. All the works were presented by the author with interesting titles, which often completed the pronunciation of the works themselves, creating hints and giving room for wide interpretations. He was also the inventor and author of many unusual artistic actions, such as the outdoor flag show in Łącek or the spectacle of the burning banners in Nowy Sącz in 1988 and 1992. He created many provocative and unconventional outdoor monuments and sculptures in many places around the world. These include: Mountain rescuers and Prometheus shot in Zakopane, a famous unrealized monument project for hostages shot in Nowy Sącz or finally Golgota in Montevideo. In his projects he broke the circular sculpture conventions, applied surprising formal solutions. An example of this is his invented technique of casting in the ground, using elements of fire and water, and even sound. His work has aroused great interest. She's been given a lot of articles and reviews. Books were written about her, master's work. A number of documentary films and television programs devoted to the artist's silhouette were filmed. For his work he was awarded with many awards, among others. the prize of the Polish Culture Foundation, and in 1999 The Academy of Fine Arts in Gdansk awarded him, unfortunately after death, the title of doctor of honoris causa. Władysław Hasior died on 14 July 1999 in Kraków. His works are currently in many Polish and European museums, among others. in Cracow, Warsaw, Łódź, Poznań, Szczecin, Wrocław, Lublin, Helsinki, Paris, Stockholm, Oslo, Säo Paulo, Rome, Milan, Amsterdam and Moscow. The second largest collection of works of Hasior has the Artistic Exhibition Office in Gorzów Wielkopolski. Many works are also found in private collections. In Nowy Sącz in the church of St. St. Kazimierz can be seen on the original cross road, one of his first works. The collection of works by Władysław Hasior also looks after the Regional Museum in Nowy Sącz, which is worth a look at when visiting the Sądecczyn, which I encourage you to do. Thank you very much, Marshal. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-04-sejm-83-2.u566	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! When the days were fulfilled and it came to die in the summer, four soldiers from Westerplatte walked straight to heaven. Many of you may remember these verses from Konstanty Ildefons Gałczyński's poem about soldiers from Westerplatte. Perhaps according to contemporary style it sounds infantile or unmodern, it does not change the fact that these verses penetrated the collective consciousness of Poles and allowed to perpetuate the myth about soldiers from Westerplatte and their heroic attitude in the first days of World War II. Today we know more about those days, we know the various fates of the formal or actual commanders of the defence squad in the port of Gdansk - Major. Henry Sucharski and Capt. Franciszek Dąbrowski. We remember the historical, archival broadcasts of the Polish Radio, which during the first September days broadcasted a message: Westerplatte still fights, thus encouraging the frightened nation, because it was a miracle that such a fragile unit resists attacks several times the enemy's number. They fought as long as they could. In recognition of General Eberhardt in charge of the German attack, he allowed Sucharski to keep an officer's sword. They were supposed to defend themselves for 12 hours, and they lasted seven days. They became a symbol of the Polish army, which always fights to the end. Do you feel, Polish, the heart rate of heroic soldiers from Westerplatte? Today, on the 80th anniversary of those events, can you feel the thrill of your heart and pride like then Poles urging your ear to a box of radios who listened again to Westerplatte defend themselves still? Can you, contemporary, recognize space and a way to honor them? If you are the same blood and the same spirit - Polish at all, appreciating honor above all and giving love to your country - then you will understand that the Gdansk peninsula is a place sanctified with blood, then and tears of a Polish soldier, over whom, if you have a sensitive ear, you will hear a constant prayer: Holy God, Holy Strong, Holy immortal, have mercy on us! From air, famine, fire and war save us, Lord! But also from the stupidity, the clumsyness in the vulgar, hooligan behavior, the national memory, the pride and honor of the nation in the mud. Let's behave ourselves, fellow countrymen, properly. Let us not forget that it is ours: Westerplatte of the Polish people. [[Applause]]

Trześniów is a village beautifully located in the Wisłoka Valley in Podkarpackie Voivodeship in the municipality of Haczów, which celebrates its 600th anniversary this year. The village founded at the end of the 14th century was first mentioned in sources written from 1419 and this date was customary to celebrate the 600th anniversary of its creation. On Sunday, June 16, the celebrations of this impressive anniversary took place. As part of them there was a Holy Mass under the leadership of Fr. Archbishop Adam Szal, the metropolitan of Przemysl, honoring the memory of the inhabitants of Trześniów who died in defence of their homeland and deserved for its development, among them General Bronisław Prugar-Ketling, a reminder of the quite colorful history of Trześniów, which was also recorded in the monograph of the village published on this occasion. There was also a letter by Marek Kuchciński's Marshal, speeches by parliamentarians and self-governments, and finally artistic performances by school youth, folk band Trześniowski weddings, orchestras of Fire Bands from Brzozów and opera singer Danuta Wójcik related to Trześniów. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-04-sejm-83-2.u572	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On July 7, 2019 we celebrate the 100th anniversary of the name given to the village of Stefanów in the municipality of Żelechów in the district of Garwolinski. The origins of the village of Stefanów are inextricably linked to German colonization. The data from the parish archives indicate that already in 1776 there was a forest settlement of Budki Francdorf inhabited by the so-called. builders, i.e. people involved in logging wood and obtaining various raw materials from wood processing. Francdorf's buildings were mainly inhabited by German colonists. Next to the Germans colonists were also settlers of Polish origin, as well as few Jews. In the '90s. Another settlers from Prussia introduced Ignacy Wyssogot Zakrzewski's property owner in the 18th century. The heir sold the Germans forests covering the territory of the current villages of Stefanów and Piasts. The colonists cut out the forest and settled here. The village was named Francdorf. In 1811 German settlers built opposite Goniwilka, on the left side of the guest house leading from Żelechów to Górzna, the first glassworks in the area, which fell after cutting out the surrounding forests. In the village census of 1827 the colony is called Fransdorf or High weather, has 80 farms and more than 600 inhabitants. In time, the colony became so vast that it was divided into two parts, and the division axis was considered the road from Żelechów to Sobolew carried out around 1880. German colonists were Evangelical-Augsburg religions. They founded a cantorate in Francdorf, which took care of elementary school, a prayer room and a cemetery. At the same time, the teacher of the primary school was an exchanger who led the services. The colony originally belonged to the Evangelical parish of Pilica behind the Vistula. The pastor from Pilica came to Francdorf several times a year. Since Żelechów's heir became Lutheran Karolina from Danglów Ordęgowa, German colonists have enjoyed great support from the gelech court. Karolina's husband, the owner of the Jewish goods Jan Ordęga, brought another craftsmen from Prussia to Francdorf. In 1833 he even submitted to the government committee on internal affairs and public enlightenment of the Podlaskie voivodship with a project to make the church of St. Stanisława in Żelechow along with the square sell to the Lutherans of Francdorf, and allocate the funds obtained to build a school in the city and a workhouse for the elderly. However, this plan has never been implemented. Colonists were leading agriculture and industry. In the area of Żelechów, they were pioneers of potato cultivation. The leader in the tannery industry in the entire Łuków district was Tomasz Maresch from Francdorf. He ran a successful tannery for those days. The years of World War I were a touching experience for German villagers. In 1915 the Tsarian authorities took away all German colonists to the depths of Russia, from where only a few returned. After the war, pursuant to the Decree of the Minister of the Interior of 7 July 1919, the name Francdorf was changed to Stefanów and Francdorf II to Piastów. The text of the Regulation states that the name of the village has been changed in accordance with the wishes of the population. There are no clear reasons why such names were chosen. During the interwar period only 13 German families lived in Stefanów, in Piastów - seven. Some families assimilated with the local population, some even changed their names. In 1946 the evangelical church of St. The Holy Trinity was transformed into a Catholic temple and incorporated into the Jewish parish. The wooden chapel received the call of Our Lady of Czestochowa. Important events in Stefanov's life, as in many villages, were electrification, voluntary fire training, construction of a milk pool, telephone and water supply, and construction of a new fire station in 2003. An important place around which the lives of several generations of Stefanov focused was the school. The current primary school dates back to 1867. My memory of Stefanov's history was based on the development of Mr. Michał Sztelmach. I would like to congratulate the people of Stefanov today on this beautiful history of the small country. I wish you the satisfaction and satisfaction of your achievements and success in pursuing your further goals, as well as any success in your personal life and building the splendor of your little homeland. Thank you very much.
